- **fin-credit**: Credit spreads, CDS mechanics and ratings migration - Z-spread against I-spread against G-spread and OAS and when they diverge, the ISDA standard model and points upfront, what TRACE actually reports and what its dissemination caps censor, and ratings migration matrices and the embedding problem that puts negative probabilities in a six-month root. Four skills, each with an executable script and a measured trap. CDS indices and tranches, default and recovery data, and portfolio loss models are planned and not here yet. ([howard-lynn-ye/fin-skills](https://github.com/howard-lynn-ye/fin-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/fin-credit) · ★1 · 4 skills)
- **fin-crypto**: Crypto market data and execution: ccxt, cryptofeed, python-binance, freqtrade, jesse, hummingbot — and the 24/7-market assumptions that break equity-derived tooling. ([howard-lynn-ye/fin-skills](https://github.com/howard-lynn-ye/fin-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/fin-crypto) · ★1 · 5 skills)
- **fin-fixed-income**: Bonds, rates and the conventions every other fixed-income number sits on - day counts and accrued interest, gilt ex-dividend windows and negative accrued, yield measures and bill discount quotes, duration convexity and DV01, SOFR compounded in arrears, LIBOR fallbacks under Regulation ZZ, and OIS multi-curve discounting. One skill per topic, each with an executable script and a measured trap. ([howard-lynn-ye/fin-skills](https://github.com/howard-lynn-ye/fin-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/fin-fixed-income) · ★1 · 7 skills)
- **fin-futures-fx**: Futures and FX: continuous-contract construction (difference vs ratio vs unadjusted and when each is wrong), roll rules as researcher degrees of freedom, contract specs and margin, CME session structure, plus FX quote conventions, pip sizing and carry — the parts an equity-shaped toolchain silently gets wrong. ([howard-lynn-ye/fin-skills](https://github.com/howard-lynn-ye/fin-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/fin-futures-fx) · ★1 · 2 skills)

- **fin-libraries**: One skill per library - fine-grained retrieval for 24 of the most-used Python quant packages, each leading with the single default or bug that silently produces wrong numbers. Set skillListingBudgetFraction to 0.03 before installing it, 0.05 to run it alongside fin-core, and 0.06 for every plugin. ([howard-lynn-ye/fin-skills](https://github.com/howard-lynn-ye/fin-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/fin-libraries) · ★1 · 24 skills)
- **fin-llm**: LLM- and ML-for-finance: agent frameworks (TradingAgents, FinRobot, RD-Agent, FinGPT), reinforcement learning and deep learning for trading, finance MCP servers, and the published evidence on whether any of it survives lookahead leakage, transaction costs, and multiple-testing correction. ([howard-lynn-ye/fin-skills](https://github.com/howard-lynn-ye/fin-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/fin-llm) · ★1 · 5 skills)
- **fin-macro**: Macro data as a point-in-time object - real-time vintages versus today's revised series, release calendars and embargo times, seasonal adjustment as a second silent vintage, recession indicators that are assigned retrospectively, nowcasting with dynamic factor models, and the non-US central-bank APIs. One skill per topic, source-verified, each with an executable script and the measured size of the trap it prevents. ([howard-lynn-ye/fin-skills](https://github.com/howard-lynn-ye/fin-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/fin-macro) · ★1 · 5 skills)
- **fin-market-data**: Getting market data and getting it right - which vendor can actually serve what you need and whether its licence lets you keep it, where prices, fundamentals and macro series come from and what each source silently gets wrong, and how to store, adjust and join them without manufacturing a look-ahead. Split out of fin-core so both stay inside the skill-listing budget; install it alongside fin-core for anything that touches data.
